# Discovery registry — `mirna_hallmark`

Tracked claims for this subproject only (independent of
`analysis/DISCOVERY_REGISTRY.md`). Strength tags:

- **S** statistical (effect + FDR-significant, confounder-adjusted)
- **P** pattern / descriptive
- **H** hypothesis (not yet tested)
- **R** reused / replicates an established external fact

Update this file whenever a run changes a finding. IDs are `MH-#`.

| id | claim | strength | evidence (output) | notes |
|----|-------|----------|-------------------|-------|
| MH-102 | **The deconvolution PANEL question is a non-issue — but *having* a confounder block is everything; and the model's C was silently MISSING one in two of its four cohorts.** Measured on **1444 (gene,family) β coefficients** (327 paired GTEx donors, 147 genes, canonical `_prep`+bagged-NNLS): β is **ρ=0.941** concordant across **entirely different single-cell atlases** (Wu-9 vs HBCA-11) and **ρ=0.986** across reference cell-subsamples (300 vs 150) — but only **ρ=0.812 (0.579 on both-nonzero edges)** vs **no C at all**, and mean\|β\| **HALVES** (0.0205→0.0099). ⇒ *which* composition control you use moves β ~10× less than *whether* you control composition. Replicated on TCGA (n=1041: S-mode pool ρ=0.997 · refsample ρ=0.918 · +CPE ρ=0.984 · no-C ρ=0.831, \|β\| −38%) and NAT (n=104: source ρ=0.957 · no-C ρ=0.729, \|β\| −44%). **CONSEQUENCES.** (i) The handoff's premise — *"reference reconciliation is the state axis's blocking first task"* — is **RETIRED**: `gtex_wu_major` exists (514, covering **327/327** paired donors), and β is panel-invariant anyway. **ZERO new CIBERSORTx runs are needed.** (ii) The REAL defect: **`state.cross_state_transfer` fits M_H with NO confounder block at all** (`_fit_M(Y,Xz)` takes only Y and X) while scoring against a C-residualised tumour target ⇒ **~half of today's "healthy wiring" signal is composition artifact**; and `cptac_validation` had **no composition term at all**. (iii) **CPE is nearly REDUNDANT given composition** (ρ=0.984 with vs without; CPE-alone ≈ no-C at ρ=0.943) and is **NOT** the malignant fraction (r=0.554; means 0.732 vs 0.235 — not substitutes) ⇒ the TCGA-CPE / CPTAC-ESTIMATE asymmetry is **benign**; no purity surgery. (iv) **A fine 42–49-type panel cannot help** — β is 0.94–0.99 invariant to reference detail, so a finer C cannot move what a whole different atlas doesn't. (v) **The run FILENAMES lie**: by md5 of each run's inferred refsample there are only **TWO** Wu references (`scref_celltype_labeled`≡`wu_major_300cells`; `scref_wu_major`≡`wu_major_150cells`≡`wu_major_C`), so the sole production mismatch is CPTAC-on-REF-B, worth β **ρ=0.986** ⇒ absorbed by the gauge + s² floor, **no harmonisation runs** (and S-mode re-adjusts per mixture regardless, so identical refsample files would not have produced identical signatures anyway). **RETRACTED en route** (all asserted before being measured): "the cross-solver disagreement is concentrated in *degenerate* compartments" (kappa is 8.1/12.0; no signature pair >0.90); "perivascular/lymphoid are unmeasured reference artifacts" (**PVL's technical reproducibility is 0.998** — the Wu-vs-HBCA anti-correlation is an *annotation/partition* difference between atlases, not noise; only `T-cells` is genuinely unstable at 0.685/0.731); "use `tcga_nat_alone` for NAT" (**reversed** — β ρ=0.957, but S-mode on NAT alone has no cancer cells to anchor the malignant signature, so it misassigns ~10% of each sample into `Cancer Epithelial` (0.033→**0.101**) straight out of `Normal Epithelial` (0.245→0.175), a real C column ⇒ **use the pooled `-NAT` rows**, which `states.py` already did); "an adipocyte metagene is mandatory for GTEx" (**default-OFF** — 69.6% explained by the existing 8 Wu fractions and **r=−0.71 with `Normal Epithelial`**, i.e. an inverse-epithelial axis ⇒ genuine over-control risk, the `data._latent` failure mode). Also: CIBERSORTx **cannot** be re-solved locally from its emitted artifacts (r=0.68) — no docker-free shortcut. | **S** (β-level measurements on 1444/1054/1875 coefficients across three cohorts; every retraction is a measurement overturning an assertion) | `learned/confounders.py` (`build_C`/`availability`); `data/external/cibersortx/README.md` (full inventory + usage rules); scratchpad `beta_invariance.py`/`tcga_c_sensitivity.py`/`settle.py`/`panel_precision.py` | The 150-cell reruns (user, 2026-07-12) supplied the missing **technical replicate** — same reference, same samples, same method, only the cell subsample — which is the *reliability ceiling* every other comparison must be read against, and is what finally separated *noise* (T-cells) from *annotation difference* (PVL). `build_C` is a verified drop-in (max\|diff\|=0.0 vs both existing consumers) ⇒ TCGA/NAT migration is a no-op. Ledger MH-102(a); plan `LEARNED_MODEL_STATE_CHANNEL_PLAN.md` §1/§3/§4/§9; board §C/§F. |
| MH-102b | **Every cross-cohort channel needs a GAUGE: `β_source = a·β_target + c + δ`. The scale is NOT 1 — not even for a same-modality, same-disease-state cohort.** `learned/gauge.py`. Calibration ladder (150 genes, TCGA-mRNA the target; **all three PASS their shuffled null**): **CPTAC-mRNA a=2.369** [1.55,3.38] ρ=0.348 · **NAT a=0.528** [0.32,0.87] ρ=0.283 · **GTEx a=0.231** [0.09,0.38] ρ=0.168. **It FACTORIZES cleanly: state (NAT — same patients, same platform) ≈2× attenuation × platform/cohort (GTEx/NAT) ≈2.3× ≈ 4.3 ≈ 1/0.231** — the platform-vs-state separation no single comparison can give. **(1) This VINDICATES `M_T = a·M_H + Δ` over the handoff's additive `M_T = M_H + Δ`:** with β_H only 23% of β_T, the additive form yields Δ>0 on nearly every edge ⇒ it would have **manufactured the state axis's own headline** ("acquired wiring") out of composition attenuation. **(2) HEADLINE FOR THE PROTEIN CHANNEL: a=2.37, not 1** ⇒ CPTAC cannot feed β_protein in as `pihat` raw; and ρ=0.348 (the best of the three) sets a realistic CEILING on how much per-edge structure any cross-cohort channel can transfer. **(3) TWO ESTIMATOR BUGS CAUGHT *ONLY* BY THE FALSIFICATION TEST:** (a) TLS **through the origin** left the shuffled null ALIVE (a_null=0.254 vs a_real=0.504) because **bagged NNLS β is ≥0 with a positive mean even on pure noise**, so an origin-forced slope reads `E[x]·E[y]>0` as signal — the shuffled *Spearman* was correctly dead (0.084), and that discrepancy is what exposed it; the centered errors-in-variables slope + intercept kills it (a_null=0.054 [−0.03,0.14], **dead**, 4.3× separation). (b) An NNLS coefficient pinned at β̂=0 across every bootstrap gets **se=0** — a boundary artifact — which with the intercept made `pihat<0` at **z≈−6**: the channel would have asserted, at 6σ, that β is NEGATIVE on **42% of source edges**, crushing real edges under the half-normal gauge. `se_floor()` fixes it (z −6→−0.72; over all 759 terms z∈[−0.74,+4.69], only 5 with \|z\|>3). Neither bug was findable by reasoning. | **S** (three independently-nulled gauges; the factorization is a quantitative decomposition) | `learned/gauge.py` (`fit_gauge`/`a_for`/`to_channel_terms`/`beta_table`/`shuffled_gauge`) | `shuffled_gauge()` is the shared **cross-cohort shuffle-null primitive** (analogue of `exclusion(shuffle_cn=)`), reused by both channels. τ is floored (`_TAU_FLOOR_FRAC`) to carry the C-construction/reference nuisance so it is not mistaken for precision. Ledger MH-102(b); plan §2/§5. |
| MH-102g | **The absolute dose-floor bug reached the CROSS-STATE arc — the GTEx-side M was silently missing a third of its regulators — and `state.cross_state_transfer` fit M_H with NO confounder block at all. Measured consequence: the GTEx-side M REORDERS — ρ(M_abs, M_free) = **ESR1 0.43** · PTEN 0.75 · CDK6 0.84 (ZEB1 1.00). Fixed by anchoring the floor to the scale it was tuned on (`x_floor = 0.1 · x_scale(state)/0.947`) ⇒ **tumour keeps EXACTLY 0.1 (zero ripple — `subtype.py` and every tumour result byte-unchanged, verified)**; GTEx → 0.027, NAT → 0.072. **(B) THE MISSING C.** `state.cross_state_transfer._fit_M` took only (Y, Xz) and merely mean-centred Y ⇒ **M_H was fit on GTEx with NO confounder block**, while the tumour target it is scored against IS C-residualised. Since no-C vs C halves |β| (MH-102, ρ=0.58), ~half its 'healthy wiring' was composition artifact. Fixed with `confounders.build_C('gtex')` (514 donors, 327/327 paired). **(C) THE PUBLISHED CLAIM HOLDS.** Re-run with BOTH fixes, the memory `parallel-view-abundance-vs-wiring` contrast survives: **ZEB1 retention 0.82 → CONSERVED (abundance-driven)** · **ESR1 retention 0.63 → STATE-DEPENDENT (wiring-driven)**, exactly as published. New: PTEN 0.27 and CDK6 −0.04, both state-dependent. **RIPPLE MAPPED:** the abundance-level cross_state/LANDSCAPE arc does **not** read the learned M ⇒ UNAFFECTED; every other `canonical_M` consumer is tumour-only (`attribution`, `families`, `calibration`, `structural_identity`) ⇒ unaffected. ⚠ **`card.py` (ΔM tumour-vs-NAT) and `program_network` read non-tumour states ⇒ their NAT-side M shifts (floor 0.1→0.072); regenerate if persisted.** | **S** (ρ(M) measurements + the re-run verdicts; tumour zero-ripple verified) | `learned/states.py` (`x_scale`/`x_floor`), `learned/state.py` (`_fit_M(C=)`), `learned/parallel_view.py` | Same bug class as MH-102d's gauge floors, found by auditing the *class* rather than the instance. Per-edge: lineage_robust 77 · deconfound 43 · unmasked_real 39 · ambiguous 18 · mechanism 13. | MH-102e | **The model's posterior widths are 35–70% TOO NARROW — and SBC would not have caught it. Plus: 20–24% of any top-100 edge list is NOT reproducible.** Two resampling diagnostics (`learned/calibration.py`), both forced by MH-102d's split-half control. **(1) POSTERIOR CALIBRATION.** Random half-cohorts have the same expected β, so `sd(β̂_A−β̂_B)/√2` is an unbiased estimate of the estimator's TRUE sampling SD; a calibrated `se` must match it. ⚠⚠ **NUMBERS CORRECTED 2026-07-12 (the scale-dependent sd-floor bug, found by the CPTAC session — see MH-102d).** The first pass used an ABSOLUTE `sd<0.2` gene floor, which on TCGA's scale (median sd 0.233) silently dropped **34% of genes** — the *low-variance* ones — so the calibration was measured on the variable two-thirds and the posterior looked far worse than it is. **CORRECTED (scale-free floor, 261 genes, 3 splits): bagged NNLS 0.73× (inflate 1.37×) · Gibbs Gaussian 0.77× (inflate 1.29×) · Gibbs Student-t ν=7 0.89× (inflate 1.13×).** ⇒ **posterior widths are ~25% too narrow, NOT 35–70%** *(the "35–70%" figure is RETRACTED)*. **AND MH-92's STUDENT-t SUBSTANTIALLY FIXES IT** (0.77 → **0.89**, i.e. nearly calibrated) — *(the earlier "t partially helps but does NOT fix it" is RETRACTED; heavy residual tails were most of the cause after all, and MH-92's ν is the right handle. Turning it on is now a live recommendation, not a wash.)* **⚠ SBC IS THE WRONG TOOL and the board item is RETIRED:** simulation-based calibration asks *is the sampler correct GIVEN the model* — it would **PASS**, and passing would mislead, because the fault is the MODEL. A correct sampler on a misspecified model still yields narrow-but-wrong posteriors. **TRIAGE (so this is not over-read).** ⚠⚠ **SHAPLEY WIDTHS ARE *NOT* AFFECTED — RE-DERIVED, and my two earlier assertions about them are BOTH RETRACTED.** A Shapley share is a **RATIO** (`γ_f β_f / Σγβ`); a *common* inflation of all β's **CANCELS** in the ratio, so β's miscalibration does not propagate to the share — neither in magnitude nor in sign. Re-derived by bootstrapping participants → refitting `channel_cn.between_family_bayes` → SD of `shap_share` (`calibration.shapley_resampled_width`): **PTEN miR-141/200a reproduces at 0.735 ± 0.441 (published 0.77±0.41 ✓), but the HONEST sampling width is 0.243 — i.e. the posterior width is 1.8× TOO WIDE (CONSERVATIVE), not too narrow.** MH-94's non-identifiability claim therefore **STANDS and is if anything under-sold**. (RETRACTED: (i) "±0.71" from naively multiplying by 1.73 — invalid, and it exceeds the [0,1] bound; (ii) "MH-94's widths are UNDERSTATED / wider makes the claim stronger" — **wrong in DIRECTION**. Both were asserted before being checked.) **The REAL Shapley defect is at the BOUNDARY:** segments where the posterior reports a share of exactly **1.000 ± 0.000** (PTEN miR-23-3p, miR-26-5p, miR-29-3p) while the bootstrap shows **0.04–0.13** genuine variability — there it claims certainty it does not have. **What IS affected:** MH-98's δ-pooling *fused width* (a variance, not a ratio) is understated; inverse-variance **FUSION WEIGHTS are SAFE** (SE *ratios* cancel); **inverse-variance FUSION WEIGHTS are SAFE** (SE *ratios* cancel); channel `s²` immaterial (~0.7% weight); and **every OOF/permutation result is UNTOUCHED** (`prolif_verdict` 2×2, discovery FDR, coupling ρ) — i.e. most headline findings stand. **(2) STABILITY SELECTION** (Meinshausen–Bühlmann; bootstrap participants → refit → top-N membership frequency). The top-100 edge ranking reproduces only **63/100 between independent half-cohorts** (Spearman–Brown puts full-cohort reliability at ~0.84, so the raw number understates); bootstrapped at full n (corrected floor: 2142 edges / 261 genes), **78/100 of the top-100 are stable (freq≥0.6) — 22 are NOT**, and 7 of the top-25 are coin-flips (NCAM1/miR-200bc 0.28, IGF1R/miR-125b 0.38, HLA-G/miR-744 0.40, KIT/miR-375 0.43). The canonical core is rock-solid (TP63/miR-130-301-454, ESR1/miR-18, EGFR + CDK6 + SPRY2/miR-21, APC/miR-17~92 all at freq 1.000). **⇒ NO top-N discovery list ships without its `freq` column.** | **S** (resampling measurements; the Student-t partial-fix and the stability frequencies are direct) | `learned/calibration.py` (`posterior_calibration`/`stability_selection`); `output/learned/{posterior_calibration,stability_selection}.tsv` | Retires the board's §E \"BN simulation-based calibration\" item as mis-specified. Ledger MH-102(e). |
| MH-102d | ⚠⚠ **NUMBERS CORRECTED 2026-07-12 (bug found by the CPTAC session).** `gauge.beta_table` used an ABSOLUTE `min_sd=0.2` variability floor, but the cohorts are on different scales (median sd(Y): **TCGA 0.233 · NAT 0.595 · GTEx 0.612 · CPTAC 1.058**) ⇒ it dropped **34% of TCGA genes but 0% of CPTAC**, and since `fit_gauge` merges on the SHARED keys the gauge was fit on *"TCGA's high-variance third"* — a biased subset. Fixed to a SCALE-FREE relative floor (`min_sd_rel=0.25` × the cohort's own median sd); the gene set grows **137 → 224** (edges ~1200 → ~2000). ⚠ **The SAME bug class was then found on the PREDICTOR side:** `attribution_eb._prep` zeroes any family with an ABSOLUTE `sd<0.1`, but the cohorts' miRNA-dose scales differ badly (median sd(X_fam): **TCGA 0.947 · NAT 0.682 · GTEx 0.235 · CPTAC 0.861**) ⇒ it zeroed **32% of GTEx's families vs 3% of TCGA's**, forcing β_H to zero for a third of the source. (That — not NNLS — was the main source of the "42% boundary zeros"; corrected, GTEx's zero-β rate falls to **24%**.) Both floors are now scale-free *inside the gauge*; `_prep` itself is unchanged (TCGA-only, 3% zeroed, no cross-cohort bias — verified, incl. per-PAM50-stratum: 2–4% everywhere). **FINAL RE-RUN — the conclusion is UNCHANGED and if anything STRENGTHENED: GTEx a=0.137, ρ=0.188, τ=0.0009, info 2.4%→0.6% · NAT a=0.326, ρ=0.277, τ=0.0221, info=0.7% · CPTAC a=0.333, ρ=0.280, τ=0.0023, info=0.7%.** τ is still indistinguishable from zero and info is now **0.6–0.7%** across all three. τ is still indistinguishable from zero and info is still ~1% ⇒ **the state channel stays CANCELLED.** ⚠ **THIRD CORRECTION:** the SHUFFLE-NULL verdict for NAT and CPTAC is **BORDERLINE and FLIPS with the gene set** (NAT's null was 'not dead' on the old set, 'dead' on the corrected one; CPTAC the reverse). GTEx's null is dead on both. ⇒ **the earlier claim that 'NAT's shuffle-null fails' is RETRACTED — it is unstable, not a finding.** **⛔ THE STATE CHANNEL IS CANCELLED, and every cross-cohort channel is informationally negligible (0.4–2.4%). The healthy→tumour per-edge wiring difference — the axis's entire deliverable — is NOT DETECTABLE.** Measured on a **calibrated** gauge (`learned/gauge.py`; split-half control `a = 1.125 [1.00,1.28]`, cal✓ — same cohort, same C, same n ⇒ truth is a=1). Under the **E3 standardized-β convention**: | source | a | ρ | τ (payload) | **info added to β_T** | shuffle-null | ⇒ **GTEx 0.112 · 0.169 · 0.0008 · 2.4% · dead✓** — **CPTAC(mRNA) 0.336 · 0.313 · 0.0024 · 0.6% · dead✓** — **NAT 0.327 · 0.287 · 0.0268 · 0.4% · ⚠NULL NOT DEAD (a_null=0.076)**. **(1) NO PAYLOAD.** τ (the spread of per-edge deviations = the acquired-vs-constitutive readout) is ~0 for GTEx and CPTAC; a simulated τ_true=0 world produces larger τ̂ by chance. NAT's apparent τ=0.027 is **retired** — it survives a matched-C refit (so it is not the C asymmetry) but it **vanishes under the Gibbs estimator** (bagged NNLS's boundary zeros give `se=0`, understating `mean(se²)`, hence overstating τ) **and its shuffle-null fails**. **(2) NO COUPLING GAIN.** The channel's precision on β_T is **0.4–2.4%** of the mRNA likelihood's own (control: a same-cohort half gives 124%). The reason is structural: precision scales as **a²**, and a≈0.11 ⇒ a²≈0.012. Even infinite GTEx donors would add ~1%. **Composition attenuation does not merely bias the healthy β — it destroys its information content.** **(3) THE HANDOFF'S FORM WOULD HAVE FAKED THE HEADLINE:** with β_H ≈ 0.11·β_T, the additive `Δ = M_T − M_H` gives Δ>0 on nearly every edge ⇒ it manufactures "acquired wiring" from attenuation. **(4) TWO GAUGE PATHOLOGIES CAUGHT BY THE CONTROLS, NOT BY REASONING:** (a) the split-half control returns **a=4.1** (3.5e6 in one config) when fed the **dense Gibbs posterior's** β/SD, where the truth is 1.0. ⚠ **CORRECTED FRAMING (this is NOT a defect in Gibbs, and an earlier claim that it was is RETRACTED): Gibbs is the BETTER estimator** — split-half reproducibility ρ=**0.822** vs bagged NNLS's **0.729** — and the **model keeps it**. The fault is *this gauge's* errors-in-variables correction, which divides by `Var(β̂)−mean(se²)`; for Gibbs `mean(se²)` is dominated by a **heavy tail of a few enormous posterior SDs** (`sqrt(mean(se²))=0.055` vs a typical se of 0.015), collapsing the denominator (reliability **0.17** vs NNLS's **0.72**). ⇒ `_MIN_RELIABILITY` + `Gauge.usable` now REFUSE the gauge instead of silently returning 4.1. **Bagged NNLS for the GAUGE; Gibbs for the MODEL.** (b) the **raw-`r` convention silently absorbed the Y-scale** — median sd(resid Y) is **TCGA 0.237 vs NAT 0.600 / GTEx 0.612** (normal tissue is ~2.5× more variable), and `a_raw/a_zY = 2.25 ≈ the sd ratio 2.58` ⇒ my earlier "healthy β is 23% of tumour β = composition attenuation" was **mostly Y-scale**. Fixed by **E3** (`zscore_y=True`, CHANNEL_FUSION §E — which the design doc had anticipated and I skipped). **(5) THE ONE ESTIMATOR-ROBUST QUANTITY is the rank concordance ρ:** split-half TCGA **0.72** (the reproducibility ceiling) · NAT→TCGA 0.27–0.39 · GTEx→TCGA 0.10–0.18 ⇒ healthy β shares ~a fifth of the reproducible per-edge structure with tumour β. That is a *relative* statement and cannot found a channel (a channel needs a scale). **⇒ `channel_state.py` NOT BUILT. The state axis (board §C) is CLOSED.** ⚠ **This lands on the PROTEIN channel too**: CPTAC-mRNA→TCGA is the *easiest possible* cross-cohort transfer (same modality, same disease state) and it caps out at **0.6%**. A protein channel at n≈100 cannot beat it on precision — it must justify itself as a **different question** (realized-at-protein repression the mRNA cannot see), never as a coupling gain. | **S** (calibrated gauge + split-half control + shuffled null + simulated τ_true=0 null; the verdict is stable across estimator × n-matching × convention) | `learned/gauge.py` (`calibrate`/`fit_gauge`/`Gauge.usable`/`info_ratio`); scratchpad `interrogate_gauge.py`, `bc_tests.py`, `e3_gauge.py` | The infrastructure was not wasted — **it is what killed the axis**, which is what falsifiable infrastructure is for. | MH-100 | **C's residual-proliferation crack is LARGE (\|ρ\|≈0.42 mean, up to 0.83) and — unlike everything else this session — a genuine coupling LEVER (β moves 20–30%); but a GLOBAL fuller proliferation control is REJECTED because it over-controls the majority, and the confound-vs-mechanism call is genuinely PER-GENE, settled by a non-circular OOF 2×2.** Resolving MH-99's model-wide finding, built `learned/prolif_verdict.py`. **(1) The crack is real & general:** `R = resid(broad cell-cycle metagene \| C)` (target-excluded) still explains **\|ρ\| up to 0.83** (effectors AURKA/BUB1/E2F1 positive; suppressors TGFBR2 −0.64 / BCL2 / PTEN / ZEB1 ≈ −0.54) of the target residual AFTER C ⇒ `mal_prolif` is materially incomplete. **(2) It is a LEVER, not a sub-σ nicety:** adding R moves couplings by median **−20 to −30%** (vs sub-σ for the CN channel MH-91 / Student-t MH-92 / gate-SEs) — the load-bearing confounder-block decision the design flagged. **(3) The effect is HETEROGENEOUS — no global or clean-stratum rule:** the SAME control de-confounds (miR-130/301/454→ZEB1 −97% while canonical miR-200bc/429→ZEB1 spared −11%), over-controls proliferation effectors (MYB 60–89%), and UN-masks real edges (let-7→CDK6 **+350%**, miR-25/92→PTEN +63% — proliferation was *hiding* them). **(4) The arbiter is a non-circular OOF 2×2** (train {C,C+R} × eval {C-space, C+R-space}): control helps both spaces → CONFOUND; hurts both → MECHANISM/over-control; flips with eval space → FRAGILE (OOF genuinely can't settle it — the disagreement is about the estimand, not predictive fit). **PANEL (28 coupled genes, repression-directed −ρ): over_control 13 (46%) · neutral 9 · confound 3 (CDK4 / THBS1 / CDKN1B) · fragile 3; mean Δ_Cspace −0.035 (net harm)** ⇒ a global fuller control over-controls the majority → **C unchanged.** The design's clean fix (AL2 intronic nascent-transcription latent) is **data-blocked** (no intronic reads). Deliverable = per-gene classifier as a **flag, not a subtraction** (sibling of MH-99's "trust only reductions"). **GENOME-WIDE (8-worker parallel, whole 1507-gene / 1242-coupled-edge universe, ~5.4 min, −ρ + live universe map):** most genes are proliferation-NEUTRAL (**923/61%** — the panel's ~50%-over_control was proliferation-ENRICHED); among the 584 affected, over_control beats confound **2.3:1** (389 vs 170; net Δ −0.017) → verdict HOLDS genome-wide. **Per-EDGE:** mechanism 472 (38%) · ambiguous 328 (26%) · prolif_robust 245 (20%) · deconfound 106 (**8.5%**) · unmasked_real 91 (**7.3%** = real edges HIDDEN by proliferation, discovery leads). **ARM-SOURCE (#2):** the confound is NOT diffuse — one arm carries it (**median concentration 0.85**), systematically the abundant arm **miR-93-5p** (106b~25) across dozens of miR-17~92 targets. **Mechanism TESTED (not assumed):** the confound is HOST-GENE transcriptional, NOT CN — miR-93/106b/25 is intronic in **MCM7** (a replication gene), and conditioning on host MCM7 removes **65–97%** of the confound vs locus CN only **8–10%** (corrects an earlier "CN-localizable" assertion → design axis **BP** intragenic-host; the handle is host mRNA, not the CN instrument). Sign-disagreeing miR-15/16 arms EXPLAIN fragile (dose-delivery §8, not a verdict split — β is family-level). **⚠ abs()→−ρ fix (2026-07-11, user-caught via the host-gene lens):** the OOF coupling used \|ρ\| (conflated repression-strengthening with sign-flips to anti-repression); now repression-directed −ρ, and all three outputs REGENERATED (headline robust; the regen also adopted the live `_arm_name_map` universe correction). | **S/P** (crack magnitude + β-lever + per-gene OOF-2×2 classification + genome-wide distribution + arm-source concentration are quantitative from real RNA / held-out prediction = S; the confound-vs-mechanism *interpretation* and the "OOF can't settle the fragile class" verdict are P/design) | `learned/prolif_verdict.py` (`gene_verdict`/`run`/`run_genomewide`/`arm_prolif_source`/`arm_source_scan`/`confound_mechanism`); `output/learned/{prolif_verdict.tsv` (28-gene 5-fold ref)`, prolif_verdict_genomewide.tsv` (1507)`, prolif_verdict_edges_genomewide.tsv` (1191)`, prolif_arm_source.tsv, prolif_confound_mechanism.tsv}`; OOF via `spike_slab.fit_gene_ss` on augmented C; `instrument.locus_cn`/`_arm_focal_locus` (CN leg) | Resolves the MH-99 model-wide finding (`mal_prolif` incomplete; proliferation confound AND mechanism). | MH-99 | **Coding-gene pleiotropy is a REAL, quantified confounder of the CN instrument (RB1@13q14 absorbs 42% of miR-16→CCND1's exclusion δ), AND probing it exposed a MODEL-WIDE finding: C's `mal_prolif` does NOT fully capture proliferation, and proliferation is BOTH confounder AND mechanism — so "control it more completely" is wrong-headed.** Built the coding-gene pleiotropy gate (CHANNEL_FUSION §H escalation step 2): for a family's CN segment, auto-enumerate co-located CODING genes and condition the exclusion `δ_s` on their expression (blocks the Z_s→CN_c→X_c→Y path). **Mechanism validated:** conditioning miR-16→CCND1 on **RB1@13q14** (co-deleted, CCND1-co-varying +0.57) absorbs **35%** (single) / **42%** (gate) of δ_s; miR-16→**BCL2** 25%. **The correct 'co-located' criterion is CN CO-AMPLIFICATION** (coding CN co-varies with the miRNA-locus CN), NOT genomic distance — RB1 is 1.7 Mb away, missed by a ±1 Mb window but recovered by CN-coamp. **⚠ MODEL-WIDE finding (the deeper result):** the data-driven candidate selection grabs **POLR2A/AURKB** (proliferation genes) that STILL correlate with the proliferation-target CCND1 **after** C's `mal_prolif` (E2F/G2M metagene) → conditioning on them *inflates* δ_s. So `mal_prolif` is INCOMPLETE (residual proliferation confounding remains in every fit), BUT the fix is NOT "more complete control" — **proliferation is simultaneously a CONFOUNDER (co-proliferation → spurious positive) AND the MECHANISM (miR-15/16→CCND1 acts through the proliferation axis; CCND1's regulated variance IS its proliferation variance)**, so over-controlling proliferation-effector targets removes the real signal. Right question = per-gene appropriateness (confound vs mechanism), not "capture better." The coding-gate over-conditioning is a *diagnostic* of proliferation-signal completeness. **SAFE GATE BUILT + PRODUCTIONIZED (`instrument.coding_pleiotropy`):** the safety backstop is NOT the OmniPath filter (which was too restrictive — RB1 is a 2-hop CCND1 regulator via RB/E2F, absent from OmniPath-direct, so it would drop the validated confounder) but the **"trust only reductions" rule** — a valid backdoor-blocking conditioning REDUCES δ_s (→ a coding `down_weight`), whereas an INCREASE is a collider artifact (X_c a collider between Z_s and Y) → IGNORE (keep δ_s). Plus a proliferation-survival candidate filter. Result: 13q14→CCND1 emits down_weight **0.44** (real, RB1/CAB39L), while the POLR2A/AURKB locus (over-conditioning) is correctly **collider_ignore→0**. Emits per-segment `coding_down_weight`∈[0,1] = the CN-channel down-weight (inflate `s²_π`), NOT a subtraction of the miRNA effect. | **S/P** (the RB1→CCND1/BCL2 coding-confound quantification + the safe collider-ignore gate is S, from real ASCAT CN + RNA; the proliferation-confound-vs-mechanism tension is a P/design finding with model-wide reach) | `instrument.coding_pleiotropy` (safe gate); `data.load_cnv_target_genes` (RB1 CN), GENCODE coords | Escalation step 2 of `CHANNEL_FUSION §2.2/§H`; the between-family (MH-88/93/94) and δ-pooling (MH-98) steps precede it. **Remaining TODOs:** genome-wide gene CN from full ASCAT (coamp filter currently Hallmark-4363-limited); revisit the proliferation-signal definition (Design §B / axis AL) per the confound-vs-mechanism tension; wire `coding_down_weight` into `exclusion`'s `s²_π` (currently a joinable side-table, not inline). Docs: `CHANNEL_FUSION §H`, memory `proliferation-confound-vs-mechanism` + `cn-instrument-gap2-arc`. |
| MH-97 | **The isomiR correction's payoff is ATTRIBUTION, not coupling magnitude — and its first result is a discovery: miR-767-5p is a PHANTOM regulator (1 RPM) whose curated ECM edges are really miR-29 5'-isomiRs.** Phase-2 gate of `ISOMIR_AWARE_MODELING` on the headline miR-29 family, using the `seed_composition` `c_{s,m,f}`: corrected `X_fam,f[s] = log2(1+Σ_m c_{s,m,f}·RPM_{s,m})`. **Donor side (miR-29 loses ~20%: miR-29a-3p 23% + miR-29b-3p 16% jump to miR-767-5p):** miR-29→ECM coupling is a **WASH** (mean Δρ +0.004, tighter 3/7) — the per-sample down-weight is ~absorbed by z-scoring (same "real-but-coupling-immaterial-at-n≈1000" pattern as the t-likelihood MH-92 / CN channel MH-91 / gate SEs). **Recipient side (the CLEAN claim is ATTRIBUTION, not coupling):** miR-767-5p's seed **GCACCAU = miR-29's +1 isomiR seed exactly** ⇒ 10/11 of its curated targets are also miR-29's (only PMP22 is miR-767-only). In BRCA miR-767 is **unexpressed (median 1 RPM** vs miR-29a 4105) — so its curated seed-edges **can only be miR-29-5'-isomiR-supplied here**; the model credits a phantom, the molecules are miR-29. That is a robust sequence+expression fact. The coupling test only *illustrates* it and is **noisy/n=1-RPM-dominated**: since the jumped-in miR-29 reads swamp miR-767's ~1 RPM, `rho_corr ≈ miR-29's own abundance coupling`, which (per MH-51) is composition-sensitive — tightens where miR-29 repression survives adjustment (**SERPINH1 +0.11→−0.24**, COL4A2/MMP2/COL3A1/SPARC) and loosens where composition-attenuated (**FBN1**, a real miR-29 target — NOT "noise") or genuinely non-miR-29 (**PMP22**); mean Δ −0.01, 5/11 tighter. ⇒ the correction **re-attributes phantom-regulator seed-edges to miR-29** (strengthens the miR-29→ECM headline) — an attribution/discovery fix, NOT a coupling-magnitude lever. Per the gate discipline it stays **default-OFF for the coupling model** (doesn't beat the held-out gate); the discovery layer is the deliverable. | **P/S** (the phantom-regulator + seed-jump attribution is quantitative from raw isoform-seq = S; the coupling gate is noisy/mixed & the recipient side is n=1-RPM-dominated = P) | `within_family.seed_composition` (`c_{s,m,f}`); miR-29/miR-767 gate (scratchpad); `LG.pooled_he_edges` (miR-767 = 11 HE edges) | Phase-2 gate of `ISOMIR_AWARE_MODELING.md §Phase 4`; builds on MH-96 (the `c` tensor). | MH-A-unit | **The learned model's last frame-level degree of freedom — the fit UNIT — resolves to A1 (per-gene): cross-gene / field pooling does NOT beat per-gene coupling at n≈1000, so "one posterior over the whole field" is not worth building.** Two cheap diagnostics (no field build). **Probe #1:** A1 genuinely leaves cross-gene identification on the table — 512/796 multi-gene CN segments (64%) are weak per-gene (F<10) yet hit ≥3 genes (27,402 tests) ⇒ the precondition for pooling FIRES (contra the design doc's "expected no"). **Probe #2 (the decisive one):** μ_m cross-gene β-pooling (`hierarchical.oof`, the A4 mechanism) is a **WASH** on a broad 40-gene sample (mean Δρ pooled−solo −0.0006, helps 19/40 coin-flip) INCLUDING the weak genes it should help most (|ρ_solo|<0.15: mean Δρ −0.0002, 5/10) — and it HURTS strong genes, worse at small n (the opposite of strength-borrow ⇒ per-gene β_g are heterogeneous, μ_m a poor shrinkage target). Since A3 (full HMC field) is warranted only if A4 pays, the cheap probe **gates out the expensive field build.** ⇒ keep A1; the fundamental frontier is not a wider fit unit but the **exogenous protein channel (CPTAC)**, the only lever left that the n≈1000 mRNA floor cannot absorb. | **S** (both probes quantitative — the weak-instrument territory from real CN first-stages, the pooling wash from held-out OOF CV; the "field doesn't pay" verdict is the falsifiable diagnostic the design asked for) | `instrument.exclusion` (probe #1); `hierarchical.oof` (probe #2, A4 pooled-vs-solo) | Resolves `CHANNEL_FUSION §6.A` (the A / fit-unit axis) — keep A1, don't build A2/A3/A4. Consistent with the program-wide "immaterial-at-n≈1000" pattern (CN channel MH-91/93, t-likelihood MH-92, isomiR refit, gate SEs). Docs: `CHANNEL_FUSION §6.A ★` + status header, ledger. |
| MH-96 | **A seed family is NOT always "one collapsed number" — a real minority have resolvable internal structure, and isomiR-seq QC flags ~a quarter of expressed multi-member families as partly mis-collapsed (a member's 5'-isomiR moves its seed off the family).** Built `learned/within_family.py` to look inside the §8 collapse along family-intrinsic axes: (a) **expression** (per member median RPM, expressed vs silent), (b) **seed-isomiR** (`seed_shift()` = per-arm 5'-isomiR fraction from RAW TCGA/CPTAC isoform-seq `isoform_coords`, locus-aware ±6nt so paralog loci ≠ seed shifts), (c) **locus-origin** (`n_active_source`). Classes: **homogeneous-collapsed / dose-partitioned (some silent) / seed-heterogeneous (mis-collapse) / all-silent**. Fetched the **full TCGA-BRCA raw isomiR cohort** (1207-file GDC manifest → `data/miRNA/GDCdata_isoform/`). **RESULTS (definitive — full TCGA cohort, 1096 Primary Tumor, PER-SAMPLE):** the fractions are computed **per sample** (fraction of the sample's within-locus mature reads NOT at the arm's global-mode 5'), mean±sd over samples ≥20 reads — **NOT pooled**, because **pooling is BIASED** (miR-181b-3p / miR-374a-3p read ~0.48 pooled but **0.007–0.010 per-sample** — pooling conflates inter-sample 5'-dominant disagreement into fake heterogeneity). Median per-sample frac **0.056** ⇒ collapse VALID for the bulk; of ~72 expressed multi-member families **21 homogeneous-collapsed / 34 dose-partitioned / 17 seed-heterogeneous** (seed-het **38→17** vs the pooled version — half were pooling false-positives). **miR-200bc/429** clean; **miR-15/16** dose-partitioned; **miR-29b/29c** seed-heterogeneous (per-sample 0.19±0.09 / 0.51±0.11). **Dispositive mis-collapse (`shifted_seed_family`):** 23/93 heterogeneous arms' dominant 5'-isomiR SEED lands in a DIFFERENT known family — **miR-29b-3p ~19% carries the miR-767-5p seed** (a headline family) — self-validated by recovering miRBase's own .1/.2 seed variants. **THE model-facing object — `seed_composition()` (per-participant `c_{s,m}` matrix, Phase 1):** 1078 participants × 2223 arms, EB-shrunk low-read cells, **matches 1078 of the model's 1079 participants by TCGA ID** (inherits the primary-tumor/normal-like-excluded cohort); miR-29b canon_frac **0.81 ± 0.08, range [0.49, 0.98]** = the per-sample variation that will move `X_fam` (a constant is absorbed by z-scoring — so the correction MUST be per-sample, not the `seed_shift` average). `family_card(gene)` folds the MH-94 PIP-entry (PTEN miR-17~92 = seed-het · co-drivers · PIP 0.99). | **S** (per-sample seed-shift + composition matrix quantitative from raw isoform-seq, full cohort, model-matched; taxonomy counts threshold-sensitive but robust) | `learned/within_family.py` (`seed_shift`/`seed_composition`/`family_structure`/`shifted_seed_family`/`family_card`), cache `output/learned/within_family/seed_shift.tsv`; `scripts/analysis/fetch_tcga_isomir.py`; raw `data/miRNA/GDCdata_isoform/` (Primary Tumor) | Module `learned/within_family.py`. Phase 0-1 of `ISOMIR_AWARE_MODELING.md` built; Phases 2-4 (isomiR-corrected `X_fam` → propagate to CN/coupling → gated) planned. The correction is per-sample (`x_corrected=c_{s,m}·x(m,s)`), NOT the per-arm average. Docs: ledger MH-96, DATA_SOURCES, memory `within-family-structure`. |
| MH-95 | **The Bayesian-Shapley reframe (continuous-PIP entry + per-draw Shapley with width) is GENERAL, not CN-specific — propagated to the identity/ownership Shapley it validates robust drivers with honest width and flags fragile ones the point estimate hides.** Built `attribution.bayes_shapley_identity`: the same identity as `identity_vs_magnitude` but its two binary/point pieces replaced from ONE inclusion (spike-slab, evidence-π) posterior — the binary `_exonerate_between` rider gate → continuous **PIP** (P(family enters); a collinear rider gets β≈0 in most draws ⇒ ~0 Shapley automatically), and the point Shapley on fixed canonical-M → **Shapley computed per posterior draw of β → posterior mean ± sd**. **RESULTS (canonical identity genes):** (1) **honest width both validates and discriminates** — **ESR1 miR-18** ownership is robust (**0.598 ± 0.022**, PIP 1.0), confirming the MH-80/86 "quiet owner" finding with tight uncertainty, whereas **PTEN miR-18 has sd 0.118 > mean 0.117** at PIP 0.495 — i.e. *genuinely cannot tell* if it owns any of PTEN (same arm, opposite reliability; the point Shapley would print a confident-looking 0.117 and hide it); (2) **continuous PIP is MORE discriminating than the binary exonerate gate** — ESR1's `_exonerate_between` kept all 7 families, but the PIP separates 3 solidly-in (1.0) / miR-1-206 borderline (0.75) / miR-17~92·miR-22·miR-221-222 marginal (0.10/0.40/0.07). This is the general "driver call carries its credible interval" upgrade (axis AQ, uncertainty propagation). | **S** (build validated on canonical genes — width-vs-reliability demonstrated ESR1 vs PTEN miR-18; the general-reframe verdict is well-grounded) | `attribution.bayes_shapley_identity`; canonical `attribution.identity_vs_magnitude` for the point comparison | Module `attribution.bayes_shapley_identity` (inclusion posterior + `spike_slab.return_samples` + per-draw `shapley_identity`). The general form the CN between-family (MH-94) was one instance of; applies equally to driver attribution. Caveat: ~min/gene (Shapley per draw) → on-demand, not genome-batch without optimization. Docs: CHANNEL_FUSION §2.5, ledger MH-95, memory `cn-instrument-gap2-arc`. |
| MH-94 | **The Bayesian continuous-inclusion form of between-family exclusion (the binary-entry→PIP + point-Shapley→posterior-Shapley replacement) is the RIGHT object — CN existence-evidence lifts entry ~10× more than it moved magnitude, and the Shapley WIDTH exposes genuine between-family non-identifiability MH-88's point estimate hid.** MH-93 closed only the magnitude/covariance half (dense π≡1, every family forced in); it left the *entry* binary. Built `channel_cn.between_family_bayes`: runs the sampler in **INCLUSION mode** (evidence-π) with vs without the CN channel → per co-located family (a) **PIP** = continuous P(family enters) — the CN lifts it through `log_L`/`B_cn` (axis **G2**, no prior/likelihood double-count since CN stays in θ), and (b) **Bayesian Shapley** of the segment reduced form `π=Σγ_fβ_f` — exact for the linear π (`φ_f=γ_fβ_f`), so the per-draw share `γ_fβ_f/Σγβ` → posterior mean ± **sd**. **RESULTS:** (1) **G2 is LESS weak than magnitude** — on ambiguous co-located families (0.15<PIP<0.85, where between-family bites) the CN moves **entry** by **ΔPIP 0.03–0.09** (CCND1 miR-17~92 0.485→0.575 (+0.091); TGFBR2 miR-18 −0.027/miR-19 +0.020), ~10× its ~0.1σ/0.7% magnitude effect — because inclusion asks the coarser "is the edge real?" a weak instrument can still answer. (2) **The genuine win = honest Shapley WIDTH** (vs MH-88's ±0.01 MC point): PTEN miR-141/200a **0.77±0.41** (truly can't tell which co-located family owns the segment), CCND1 miR-182/96 0.88/0.12 **±0.33** — the between-family non-identifiability made explicit. **GENERAL (not CN-specific):** the reframe — binary coalition entry → inclusion `z_f`/PIP; point Shapley → per-posterior-draw Shapley with width — applies to **every** Shapley in the program (identity_vs_magnitude, between-family, driver attribution); CN is merely one channel informing the posterior. | **S** (build validated on the canonical clusters — CN existence-lift quantified + honest width demonstrated; the "G2 less-weak-than-magnitude + general-Shapley-reframe" verdict is well-grounded) | `channel_cn.between_family_bayes`; scratchpad `g2_probe.py` | Module `channel_cn.between_family_bayes` (inclusion mode + `return_samples`). Corrects MH-93's over-stated "closed" (magnitude half only). Design/verdict `CHANNEL_FUSION §2.5`; ledger MH-94, memory `cn-instrument-gap2-arc`. Remaining Gap-2B Bayes = within-family δ-pooling (§6-F, the general same-seed-arm resolver). Backward-compatible. |
| MH-93 | **The cross-family posterior-covariance form of between-family exclusion is correct and directionally right, but WEAK — the between-family split is mRNA-likelihood-dominated; the CN anchor adds only its ~0.7% exogenous slice.** Built `learned/channel_cn.py` (`multi_family_terms`): groups a gene's families' arms into **genomic clusters** (shared CN segment `Z_c`) and emits ONE `_gibbs_posterior` channel term per cluster with `members=[(col_f, γ_{f,c})…]`, `π̂=Z_c→r`, `s²_π=se²+δ²` — the multi-member `e_s=π̂−Σ_{other}γβ` path induces the **cross-family posterior COVARIANCE** = between-family exclusion with honest width (unifies the MH-91 single-family MVP; added `return_samples=` to read it). **GAUGE FIX (axis E, make-or-break):** `π̂` must be on the **RAW `r=−resid(Y)` scale** (z-score only `Z`+`X`, matching `_prep`) — a z-scored-`r` first cut put `π̂` ~6× off and silently killed the channel; verified IV-β(`π̂/γ`)≈standardized obs-β on clean single-family repressors **both on the `r` convention** (positive=repression, gaps 0.02–0.08; miR-103 0.150/0.226, miR-181 0.169/0.208), the sign-diffs (miR-205 IV +0.15/obs −0.10) being pleiotropic gate-flagged edges not sign bugs. **The r-scale is a pure rescaling ⇒ standardized |Δβ|/σ is gauge-INVARIANT** (raw 0.0144 vs z-scored-r 0.0140, corr 0.99), so the MH-91 weak verdict is scale-independent and the fix only makes `channel_cn` consistent with production `_prep`. **Falsification:** nests §6b (channel off = bit-identical) ✅; gauge ✅; shuffled-CN null collapses the term ✅; **resolves the collinear cluster = directionally YES, materially NO** — on PTEN/CCND1 miR-17~92/106b-25 clusters the channel adds cross-family anti-corr (−0.068→−0.069) + shifts the between-family share (~0.49→0.48) by only **~0.7% weight (~0.1σ)**. ⇒ the design's "Bayes covariance *replaces* MH-88's point Shapley" is **half right**: the Bayes form gives honest *width*, but the CN doesn't materially improve the *resolution* (MH-88's abundance-conditioning Shapley is the stronger resolver; CN is its exogenous-but-weak complement). **Between-family exclusion CLOSED as a Bayes build** (correct/validated/weak). | **S** (build validated on the falsification ladder — nesting/gauge/shuffled-null — + quantified weak effect on the canonical clusters; the "correct-but-mRNA-dominated" verdict is well-grounded) | `learned/channel_cn.py`; `spike_slab._gibbs_posterior(return_samples=)`; scratchpad `xfam_bench.py` | Modules `learned/channel_cn.py` (new), `spike_slab._gibbs_posterior` (+`return_samples`). | MH-92 | **The dense §6b mRNA likelihood is heavy-tailed (Student-t df≈7), NOT count-like — so the honest upgrade is Student-t, not NB; but at n≈1000 it is a correctness/robustness upgrade, not a performance lever.** Ran the dense-mRNA residual diagnostic (the WHATS_NEXT-① flagged first step; 85 genes × 1041 patients, exact `spike_slab._gibbs_posterior` π≡1 fit): pooled standardized residuals have **excess-kurtosis 3.5** (median **1.2** across the 82 well-expressed genes), **Student-t MLE df≈7**, tails **P(\|z\|>4)=33×** Gaussian. Two mechanisms — **amplified-subset** tumours (+skew, ERBB2 +0.7 / ERBB3 +4.9 = a patient mixture) and **near-floor** genes (−skew, only 3, essentially unexpressed, HNF4A kurt 112). **NB is rejected:** no low-expression count gradient (**Spearman(expr, kurtosis)=+0.19**, the *wrong* sign for NB; the near-floor genes are a detectability-floor issue). **BUILT** Student-t in `_gibbs_posterior(nu=)` via a **scale-mixture of normals** (`ε_i~N(0,σ²/λ_i), λ_i~Gamma(ν/2,ν/2)`; per-obs λ_i Gibbs step) — **stays Gibbs, no HMC**; `nu=None` bit-identical to §6b. **VALIDATED:** nests Gaussian (Δβ≈0.001 at ν=1e9), **robust 1.76× lower error** under 5% gross outlier contamination, but **OOF coupling Δρ=+0.001 (n.s., Wilcoxon p=0.15)** and attribution unchanged (corr **0.999**, PTEN top-5 driver overlap 5/5) ⇒ correctness/robustness, not a lever, at n≈1000 (same "principled-but-immaterial-at-this-depth" shape as CN channel MH-91). Also wired **CN-channel t** (`t['nu']` — discounts a collider-outlier segment, β 0.535→0.508) + optional **exclusion-gate SE bootstrap** (`instrument.exclusion(n_boot=)`) that **confirms the analytic Sobel SEs are adequate** (s²_π agrees 0.94–1.11× on both anchors — s²_π is **δ²-pleiotropy-floor-dominated** on weak/pleiotropic segments). | **S** (residual diagnostic 85 genes/88k residuals + implementation validated on nesting/robustness/OOF/attribution; the "t not NB, robustify-not-lever" verdict well-powered) | `spike_slab._gibbs_posterior(nu=)`, `instrument.exclusion(n_boot=)`; scratchpad `dense_resid_diag.{py,tsv}` / `t_validate.py` / `t_oof.py` | Modules `spike_slab._gibbs_posterior` (+`nu`, +channel `t['nu']`), `instrument.exclusion` (+`n_boot`). Non-Gaussian count/Beta/saturation channels (protein/binding/methylation) reserved for HMC (axis J) when they land. Backward-compatible (existing callers = Gaussian default). |
| MH-91 | **The CN channel-fusion works and is directionally correct, but its effect on the fused posterior is quantitatively WEAK — the CN's real value is DIAGNOSTIC, not a broad β-mover.** Built the exogenous-channel MVP: `exclusion`'s per-segment `(pi_causal, s²_π, γ_s)` wired into `spike_slab._gibbs_posterior(channels=…)` as the conjugate term `A_cn=γ²/s²_π, B_cn=γ·e_s/s²_π` (`π̂_s ~ N(Σ_f γ_{f,s}β_f, s²_π)`, CHANNEL_FUSION §1). **Verdict (6 cluster-genes, 234 channel-families):** the channel moves β **10× more on flat/collinear families** (mean \|Δβ\| 0.0030) than already-identified ones (0.0003) — the design intuition (acts where the mRNA can't identify) holds — but the magnitude is **~0.2σ** (\|Δβ\|≈0.003 vs mRNA-sd 0.017) with **~zero posterior tightening**; material on only a handful of edges (max \|Δβ\|=0.049, TGFBR2). **Root cause (fundamental, not a function-choice):** the IV precision `A_cn = γ²/s²_π ≈ γ²·A_mRNA`, and γ (first stage, CN→abundance) is small (~0.2) ⇒ only ~γ²≈4% of exposure variance is CN-driven ⇒ the IV is ~25× weaker than the n=995 observational OLS (F=150 = γ *significantly* ≠0 ≠ γ² *variance-explained* = small). `β·γ` IS the right object (= 2SLS, full exogenous info); the weakness is intrinsic to the small exogenous slice. So the channel-fusion is the exogenous-data completion of the §6b "priors don't move coupling" result. | **S** (mechanism verified + null-validated gate + quantified weak effect, 234 families; the "diagnostic not β-mover" verdict is well-powered) | `spike_slab._gibbs_posterior(channels=)`; `output/learned/instrument/exclusion.tsv`; flat-direction test (scratchpad) | Modules `spike_slab._gibbs_posterior(channels=)`, `instrument.exclusion` (MH-90). The CN's value = the exclusion GATE (which edges are causally-clean) + the `pi_causal` pleiotropy correction, not the fused β. Next (fixed-n ceiling): a larger cohort (METABRIC-full, EGA-gated) raises `s²_π∝1/n`; multi-IV pools segments (marginal, k small). |
| MH-89 | **The CN instrument's exclusion restriction FAILS for the majority of its own well-instrumented edges — per-edge causal validity is locus-specific and much weaker than F>10 implies.** A per-edge **T1 mediator-conditioning screen** (does CN→target survive conditioning on the arm's own abundance? survives ⇒ a direct/pleiotropic non-arm path ⇒ invalid; `pleiotropy_frac = π_cond/π_raw`) over the 403 testable well-instrumented edges (F>10, n≥200): **only 108 (27%) are exclusion-clean (frac<0.4); median frac = 0.75; ~54% pleiotropic (frac>0.7)** → "F>10" overstates the causal-usable set ~4×. **Validity is locus-specific:** canonical **miR-16→CCND1 FAILS** (frac 0.91 — its locus is the chr13q14 RB1/DLEU cell-cycle CN hotspot; T1 0.242→0.220; RB1↔CCND1 ρ=+0.57 absorbs ~23%; MH-88 exonerates the miR-15/16 family entirely on CCND1), while **miR-92a→PTEN PASSES** (frac 0.16 — 13q31 amp hits PTEN *through* the miRNA). **Gauge/scale is fine** (on the clean miR-92a→PTEN, IV-β +0.319 ≈ observational +0.273, gap 0.045 vs 1.45 on the pleiotropic edge) — the binding constraint is exclusion, not scale. ⇒ CN-channel admission needs `F>10 **AND** T1-clean`; MH-88's miRNA-only between-family exclusion is **insufficient** (misses coding-gene pleiotropy). | **S** (quantitative screen, 403 edges; caveats: (i) T1 is CONSERVATIVE — conditioning on the mediator `X_fam` opens a collider path for any shared program `U` not fully in `C`, biasing toward "pleiotropic", so **27%-clean is a LOWER BOUND**; (ii) the robust readout is the absolute residual `δ_s` + SE, not the ratio `frac` (which inherits the Wald small-`π_raw` instability — the "frac>1/4.99" cases are ratio artifacts, not a distinct collider class); (iii) the collider-FREE test is **over-ID / Hansen-J** — already in `family_multi_iv` — primary where a family has ≥2 independent-CN segments, complementary to T1 (over-ID catches heterogeneous, T1 catches homogeneous co-amplification-hotspot pleiotropy); the screen ran T1 only, so an over-ID pass would refine the count) | scratchpad probes (`egauge.py`/`eresolve.py`/`t1screen.py`) + **PRODUCTION gate BUILT (MH-90): `instrument.exclusion`/`run_exclusion` → `output/learned/instrument/exclusion.tsv`** (45k segment-rows; CONTINUOUS `(pi_causal, s²_π=Sobel+δ_s², γ_s)` per segment, over-ID context; anchors verified 689 vs 21; universe 25% clean/75% pleiotropic = the screen), design `LEARNED_MODEL_CHANNEL_FUSION_DESIGN.md §2.1/§2.2` | **Caveats MH-87/88:** the CN Ring-1 + between-family exclusion are sound where the instrument is valid, but validity holds for only ~27% of well-instrumented edges — the CN channel's honest support is small and must be earned per-edge. | MH-88 | **Ring-1's per-segment CN causal share `p_S` is between-family EXCLUSION-ROBUST for ~99% of well-instrumented HE families; the co-located other-seed confound is real but quantitatively small and concentrated on the polycistronic oncomiR clusters.** Gap 2B frequentist interim (`instrument.between_family_exclusion`, CLI `--gap2b`): for each seed family × gene, enumerate co-located OTHER-seed co-targeters via `pleiotropy()`, **condition** each CN segment's reduced form on their pooled abundance (`seg_pi_cond`, blocking the CN→other-family→target mediation) and **attribute** ownership among {our family}∪{co-located families} with the between-family exoneration gate + Shapley (`attribution._exonerate_between` + `shapley_identity`). **Universe (1,436 HE genes → 4,922 (gene,family)):** **4,489 (91%) `between_clean`** (no co-located other-seed co-targeter → exclusion trivially holds); of the **433 confoundable**, **236 `robust`** / 25 `partly_attenuated` / 13 `confounded` / 63 `confounded_rider` / 96 `weak_instrument`. **Median \|pi_shift\| over confoundable F>10 segments = 0.007** (90th pctile 0.053) — conditioning barely moves `p_S`. The confound bites exactly where biology predicts: the miR-17~92 / 106b~25 cluster on **CCND1** (pi −0.156→−0.073, halved; family keeps 56% Shapley ownership), **PTEN** (52%), **TGFBR2/MDM2/BCL2L11/MYC**, and same-cluster passenger **miR-18a→CCND1** (`confounded`: pi collapses under conditioning, yet abundance-Shapley=1.0 → its CN effect is the cluster's, mediated through its neighbours). Even in worst cases the family retains ~majority ownership. | **S/P** (91%-clean + median \|pi_shift\|=0.007 aggregate = S, quantitative; per-family verdicts + between-family Shapley ownership = P, descriptive) | `output/learned/instrument/between_family_exclusion.tsv` (10,193 segment-rows) | Modules `instrument.{between_family_exclusion,run_between_family_exclusion}`, wiring `attribution.{_exonerate_between,shapley_identity}` to the CN reduced form. Design `CN_INSTRUMENT.md §9` (Gap 2B). **Frequentist INTERIM** — conditions on the co-located families' *abundance* (mediation adjustment); the exogenous CN-anchor form (CN reduced form as anchor on the cross-family posterior covariance) + within-family δ-pooling is the parked **Bayes unification** (next arc). Remaining Gap-2B siblings: coding-gene pleiotropy (OmniPath/DepMap). Builds on Ring-1 (MH-87). **⚠ MH-89 caveat:** the T1 exclusion screen shows this miRNA-only between-family exclusion is INSUFFICIENT for most edges — coding-gene pleiotropy (e.g. RB1 at 13q14) is a bigger part of the confound; per-edge CN validity holds for only ~27% of well-instrumented edges. |
| MH-86 | **The attribution-IDENTITY model (who is DESIGNED to own gene g, abundance-removed) is a validated DESCRIPTIVE model but earns NO value in the TCGA regression — identity/specificity is inert on every model job.** Built a two-level identity readout (`attribution_identity.py`): **L1 family ownership** = softmax of the affinity-PERCENTILE (is g among the seed-family's strongest TargetScan context++ targets; seed-family-keyed via `seq_specificity.seed_family_map`; K_D checked-worse ⚠ **OVERTURNED — see MH-87** (HE-restricted only; genome-wide K_D beats context++)) + **L2 arm distribution** from pooled chimeric provenance (`chimeric_evidence.py`, Manakov+TarBase overlap-resolved). Each of the **11 curated TAD-panel breast lines** (reliable labels) Spearman-correlated to the 4 centroids → best-match vs nominal. **Results: (mRNA) cell lines track subtype** — **coarse Luminal/HER2/Basal = 100%** (MCF7/T47D→Luminal, SKBR3→HER2, MDA-MB-231/468→Basal all correct), fine 73% (only LumA↔LumB slips), mean own-subtype ρ **+0.18** (all 11 positive). **(pressure) cell lines do NOT** — fine 27%, coarse 73%, mean own-subtype ρ **−0.01** (T47D −0.22, MCF7 −0.09, MDA-MB-231 −0.05; cross-class errors MDA-MB-231 Basal→LumA, SKBR3 HER2→LumA). **The disambiguating control — TCGA tumour-only 50/50 split:** mRNA held-out subtype accuracy **76%** (own ρ 0.31) AND **pressure 66%** (own ρ 0.23, ≫ chance 25%) ⇒ **miRNA-pressure DOES separate subtypes among tumours** on the same 543-gene space; it is NOT subtype-uninformative. So the cell-line pressure collapse is specifically the **cell-line miRNA measurement (NanoString)**, not the pressure construct nor the feature space — the exact weak link MH-67's EMT-gate flagged, now shown orthogonally. **Takeaways:** (a) breast cell lines are valid tumour-subtype models **transcriptionally** (use mRNA-layer claims freely); (b) the framework's **pressure is subtype-discriminative in tumours** (a positive validation of the pressure axis); (c) cell-line miRNA-pressure analyses inherit the NanoString limitation and should be read with that caveat. | **S/P** (TCGA held-out pressure subtype-separation 66% = S; small-n cell-line fidelity = P) | `cell_line_subtype_fidelity/{method_manifest.json, line_subtype_match.tsv}` | Module `cell_line_subtype_fidelity.py`; TCGA `load_rna`/`compute_gene_pressure` + DepMap RNA + CCLE NanoString miRNA→pressure; curated labels via `cell_line_subtype_map` (TAD panel). Caveats: 11 curated lines (set-level read); LumA/LumB intrinsically hard (collapsed to coarse); pressure feature space 543 genes (but the TCGA control uses the same space → adequacy shown). Directly answers the cell-line↔subtype cross-modal question; complements MH-67. |

Per-miRNA FDR: abundance 3 / anti-corr 5 / escape 5; median -log10p 0.42 / 0.75 / 0.69. ⇒ functional/dysfunction readouts beat abundance; escape opens a thin TCGA crack; still Buffa-dominant → METABRIC-full is the powered test. | **B** (functional-panel FDR beats abundance; escape opens TCGA; Buffa-dominant) | `outcome_escape_signature/{escape_signature.tsv, method_manifest.json}` | Module `outcome_escape_signature.py`. |
| MH-67 | **Phase 4c, breast-retargeted (the K562→breast fix): the framework's edges are NOT anti-correlated across the CCLE breast cell-line panel — but a failed internal positive control shows the CROSS-CELL-LINE axis itself is insensitive to miRNA repression, so this is INCONCLUSIVE, not a refutation.** Built `ccle_breast_target_anticorr.py` to replace leukemia-K562 (MH-65) with **breast + subtype + power**: tested the 1,013 headline edges as miRNA↔target Spearman **across 50 CCLE breast lines** (matched 2018 NanoString miRNA + DepMap 26Q1 RNA-seq; intrinsic subtypes **Basal 22 / HER2 21 / Luminal 7**), overall + per-subtype + subtype-adjusted (n=50 ≫ the K562 n=19, so per-edge FDR is feasible). **Result: edges are POSITIVELY correlated** (903 testable, median ρ **+0.046**, 63% positive, **0 FDR-neg**; subtype-adjusted +0.055; per-subtype Basal +0.042 / HER2 +0.058; no decoy specificity, cognate +0.046 vs decoy +0.027; **TCGA sign-concordance only 0.37**). **TWO controls make it DOUBLY inconclusive — not a refutation:** **(i) positive control fails** — gold-standard repressive pairs (miR-200c→ZEB1/ZEB2, let-7→HMGA2, miR-21→PDCD4/PTEN, miR-10b→HOXD10) are **also not recovered** (median ρ **+0.07**, 30% neg) ⇒ the cross-line axis (between-line variance) is **insensitive to repression** (co-regulation/lineage dominates — a known CCLE property; exactly why the framework uses WITHIN-cohort partial correlation). **(ii) miRNA data-validity gate FAILS** — asked whether the NanoString miRNA even recovers known EMT identity: miR-200c/141 (canonical **epithelial** markers) are **flat/inverted** across known epithelial vs mesenchymal lines (miR-200c epi median **6.16 ≤ mes 7.38**, p=0.11 wrong direction), while the **DepMap mRNA EMT markers are textbook** on the SAME lines (ZEB1 epi 0.30 vs mes 4.22; CDH1 7.68 vs 0.85; VIM 2.20 vs 10.68). ⇒ the **2018 NanoString miRNA is itself the broken link** — so the cross-line null is *measurement-confounded on top of being the wrong axis*. **Follow-up checked:** a cleaner miRNA-**seq** swap would help, but **no large (n≫10) breast-line small-RNA-seq matrix matched to mRNA exists** (CCLE miRNA = the NanoString panel; GEO breast-line miRNA-seq panels are ~9 lines → no cross-line power). Net (with MH-65): **neither measured route can deliver the test** — K562 per-cell too thin, CCLE breast cross-line has both wrong axis AND broken miRNA — so the framework's repression evidence remains the within-sample partial-correlation design + spatial localization (MH-64/66); a measured *per-cell breast* test stays the open gap (Phase 4b). | **P** (clean cross-line null, gated to inconclusive by BOTH a failed positive control and a failed miRNA-validity check) | `ccle_breast_target_anticorr/{method_manifest.json, edge_ccle_breast_anticorr.tsv, positive_control_pairs.tsv, family_ccle_summary.tsv}` | Module `ccle_breast_target_anticorr.py`; data `data/CCLE/{CCLE_miRNA_20181103.gct, OmicsExpressionTPMLogp1HumanProteinCodingGenes.csv}` (DepMap 26Q1, newly staged). Subtype from DepMap `ModelSubtypeFeatures`. Non-circular. **Honest read: a null here says nothing about the framework — the axis can't see miR-200→ZEB1 AND the NanoString miRNA can't even mark epithelial identity.** Complements, does not replace, MH-65. **Sensitivity (`--curated`, the 11-line TAD-aligned hand-curated panel, reliable labels):** restricting to the well-characterised lines does NOT rescue it — EMT gate still FAILS (miR-200c epi 6.16 vs mes 9.26, more inverted), positive control worse (median +0.34), edges still positive → confirms the broken link is the **NanoString miRNA measurement**, not line selection. |
| MH-67 | **Roadmap Phase 3 outcome — famous-miRNA head-to-head: FUNCTIONAL repression (anti-corr) out-prognoses raw ABUNDANCE; pressure sharpens the miR-200 EMT signal.** Per famous miRNA, three per-patient readouts over its own BY-neg targets — abundance (expression), anti-corr (-mean z target expression = realized repression), pressure (framework gene-pressure on targets) — Cox vs outcome. **anti-corr beats abundance 42/68 (62%) head-to-head, more FDR hits (5 vs 3), stronger median signal (-log10p 0.75 vs 0.42)** ⇒ *whether the miRNA actually represses its targets* prognoses better than how much is present. **Pressure gives the most FDR hits (10) but concentrated in the correlated miR-200/429 family** (200b HR 1.60 / 200c 1.49 / 200a 1.42, q<0.01 — the EMT-suppressor axis). Both beat abundance. **All Buffa/DRFS (TCGA null)** — within-METABRIC, where miRNA-prognosis signal exists. | **B** (clear ordering anti-corr>abundance; pressure→miR-200; Buffa-only) | `outcome_famous_compare/{famous_compare.tsv, method_manifest.json}` | Module `outcome_famous_compare.py`. |
| MH-66 | **Phase 3 spatial, hardened — the brake-release genes physically CO-OCCUR with their programs in space, and an inferCNV malignancy axis shows the concordant-repression arm is a healthy→tumour TRANSITION signal, not an intratumoral-grade one.** Two pushes on MH-64. **(Push #2 — neighbourhood co-occurrence, `spatial_coexpression_niche`):** the Finding-3 brake-release co-localizations are not just global across-spot correlations — **bivariate Moran's I** (kNN, 199-perm location null) is positive and **permutation-significant in ALL 7 primary Visium sections**: SLC2A1↔glycolysis I=0.164, FAP↔EMT I=0.142, VIM↔EMT I=0.232, HIF1A↔hypoxia I=0.070 → the released-pressure genes and the programs they serve occupy the **same physical neighbourhoods**, not merely co-vary section-wide. **(Push #1 — within-section malignancy axis, `spatial_malignancy_retest`):** built an **inferCNV-style per-spot malignancy score** (genome-wide CNV energy vs a marker-defined diploid stroma/immune reference; validated — within epithelial spots malignancy tracks proliferation ρ 0.30–0.69 across 7 sections) as a *within-section* malignant→normal-epithelium axis, the primary-invasive analogue of `delta_tumor_gtex` on the mRNA side. **The brake-release arm REPRODUCES on primary invasive tissue** — SLC2A1 rises with malignancy (median ρ +0.087, **+0.075 with its own chromosome 1 removed**, so not its own CNV; modal class brake_release across 7 sections). **But the concordant-repression arm does NOT reproduce on this axis** — the gained-pressure epithelial genes ERBB2 (+0.125), ESR1 (+0.070), CDH1 (+0.080) **rise** with intratumoral malignancy (discordant_rise, own-chr-removed unchanged), the OPPOSITE of their fall on the MIBI normal→DCIS→IBC stage axis (MH-64). ⇒ **concordant-repression is specifically a healthy→tumour *differentiation/transition* phenomenon (the normal→invasive stage axis), not an intratumoral CNV-grade phenomenon** — the two axes are biologically distinct, which sharpens what `delta_tumor_gtex` actually encodes. Brake-release (SLC2A1) holds on both axes; only the repression arm is axis-specific. | **S** (neighbourhood co-occurrence, perm-significant ×7) / **P** (malignancy-axis retest, honest axis-specificity) | `spatial_coexpression_niche/{neighbourhood_cooccurrence.tsv, per_section_bivariate.tsv}`, `spatial_malignancy_retest/{anchor_malignancy_retest.tsv, malignancy_retest_pooled.tsv, malignancy_retest_per_section.tsv.gz}` | Modules `spatial_coexpression_niche.py`, `spatial_malignancy_retest.py`; engine `spatial_common.{bivariate_morans_i, infercnv_malignancy, load_gene_positions}`. **Caveats:** malignancy is mRNA-derived CNV-from-expression (approximate; reference = marker-low-epithelial spots; CID sections TNBC-enriched so HER2 amplification largely absent) — still no spatial miRNA, the pressure side stays the projected bulk delta. The malignancy axis ≠ a true normal-tissue contrast (no truly-normal epithelium dominates a tumour section), which is *why* concordant-repression doesn't appear and is the honest point, not a failure. FAP brake-release is flat on the epithelial-restricted malignancy axis (it is stromal — expected). Bivariate I is symmetric (co-occurrence, not direction). |

| MH-65 | **Roadmap Phase 4c — the ONLY MEASURED per-cell miRNA↔target test that exists is too thin to confirm or refute the framework: INCONCLUSIVE, honestly reported.** Acquired GSE114071 (Wang 2019 half-cell co-seq): **19 phenotypically-identical K562 cells**, each physically split → one half small-RNA-seq (miRNA), other half RNA-seq (mRNA) — the only data where a miRNA and its target mRNA are *measured in the same single cell* (no deconvolution, no inference). Pairing validated: the two .gct column labels carry the physical **HalfCell** id; miRNA half-cells {1-5,7-20,23,24} ∩ mRNA {1-21} = exactly **19** (matches the paper's stated n). Tested the framework's **1,013 headline BY-neg edges** as per-cell Spearman across the 19 cells. **(1) Headline edges = NULL:** 229/1013 testable (miRNA arm expressed + target detected), **51% negative, median sc_ρ −0.009, binom p=0.40**; no decoy specificity (cognate −0.009 vs same-arm-non-target decoy −0.009, MWU p=0.46). **(2) But the MAX-POWER positive control — abundant miRNA → broad curated targets vs decoy — is only WEAKLY directional, NOT significant** (cognate −0.016 vs decoy −0.004 over 2,741 vs 24,465 pairs, MWU p=0.17) ⇒ the binding limit is the **data, not the framework**: 19 cells × single-half-cell dropout × log2-floored miRNA (median arm sits at the floor; only 89 arms expressed). **(3) The one arm with real dynamic range carries the expected sign:** **miR-92a-3p** (~3 log2 above the next-abundant) shows median target ρ=−0.040 over 377 expressed targets; abundant-arm-restricted cognate edges median −0.043 (n=66). Net: executing the only measured per-cell route returns an honest **inconclusive** — it neither confirms nor refutes the pressure axis at this resolution, and empirically demonstrates why the route is bounded → **motivates the Phase 4b sorted/LCM small-RNA ask**. | **P** (directional, underpowered; cleanly null at face value, inconclusive under positive-control gating) | `sc_mirna_target_k562/{method_manifest.json, edge_sc_anticorr.tsv, abundant_arm_target_rho.tsv, family_sc_summary.tsv}` | Module `sc_mirna_target_k562.py`; data `data/external/sc_mirna_mrna_gse114071/` (GSE114071). Non-circular (independent platform/resolution/cell type from the miRTarBase+TCGA edges). **Caveats:** **K562 (CML), not breast** — generalization-bounded by design; n=19 → set-level only, never per-edge FDR; miRNA arm-level (precursor paralogs collapsed, seed-family members not individually resolvable). The clean face-value null must be read THROUGH the failed-to-reach-significance positive control: this is "too little measured data," not "framework refuted." Routes 4a (SiCmiR inferred, circular) / 4b (sorted-LCM, acquisition) remain. |

(3) **RSF 5-fold CV:** pressure+clin **worse** than clinical in TCGA (DFI 0.55 vs 0.63; OS 0.60 vs 0.69 — strong TNM+age baseline), **better** in Buffa (0.63 vs 0.54 — but ER-only weak baseline). ⇒ even state/configuration/nonlinear reframes give no robustly-replicated prognostic signal; the **coherence→OS TCGA hit is the one nominal nugget for follow-up (METABRIC-full)**. up to 0.78 per section). **(2) MH-56d reproduces in situ** on the MIBI normal→DCIS→IBC progression axis (epithelial-lineage cells; the in-situ analogue of `delta_tumor_gtex`): **SLC2A1/GLUT1 + FAP = brake-release** (pressure released → protein rises at invasion), **ERBB2/HER2, ESR1/ER, CDH1/ECAD = concordant-repression** (pressure gained → fall at invasion), **HIF1A, VIM = discordant-rise** (pressure gained yet rise — the overridden brake) — i.e. the exact gene-by-gene split MH-56d called in bulk, now at single-cell-protein resolution (engine-validation gate passes 3/3). **(3) Brake-release is spatially real in PRIMARY tumours:** the released-pressure genes co-localise with the programs they serve — **SLC2A1 → glycolysis** (ρ 0.30 Wu / 0.27 10x) and **FAP → EMT** (ρ 0.24 Wu / 0.32 10x) across all 7 primary Visium sections. ⇒ decoupling is **gene-specific, not wholesale, and spatially organised** — programs run as compartment niches whose miRNA brakes are selectively released (SLC2A1/FAP) or overridden (HIF1A/VIM) rather than uniformly coupled. | **P** (cross-resolution localization concordance) / **S** (in-situ MH-56d reproduction + brake-release co-localization) | `spatial_synthesis/{cross_resolution_localization.tsv, cross_resolution_morans.tsv, decoupling_verdict.json}`, `spatial_mibi_anchor/{mh56d_spatial_retest.tsv, program_localization_by_stage.tsv}`, `spatial_visium_wu/{anchor_brake_release_localization.tsv, program_compartment_localization.tsv}`, `spatial_visium_public/`, `spatial_xenium/program_by_compartment.tsv` | Modules `spatial_common.py` (engine: `gene_level_pressure_retest`, `analyse_spots`, `deconvolve_matrix`, `morans_i`; At unit weights (γ=0.5) **382/783** multi-regulator genes reassign the top arm (dominant axis: breast 146, negative-evidence 111, sequence 77, guide/passenger 48); all guide/passenger flips correctly demote a star arm to a guide (e.g. IRAK2 miR-15a-3p→miR-16-5p), 0 violations.
